Right now, most people think AI is justabout chatting with a bot, generatingimages, or asking ChatGPT to write somecode. But, the AI industry is moving farbeyond that. We are now entering the eraof agentic AI, AI systems that don'tjust respond to prompts, but canactually plan tasks, make decisions, usetools, remember context, and retrieveinformation almost like a digitalemployee. And this shift is happeningincredibly fast. According to industryreports, the global agentic AI market isexpected to grow well above 100 milliondollars within the next decade. That'swhy companies today are racing to buildAI agents for customer support, researchautomation, coding, workflow management,health care, cyber security, andbusiness operations. So, without wasting any time,let's get started.Right now, most people think AI is justtools like ChatGPT answering questions.You type a prompt and the AI gives aresponse and that's it. But, the nextwave of AI is very different. Imagine anAI that doesn't just answer yourquestions, it plans tasks, makesdecisions, and completes work on itsown. Instead of being just a tool, itstarts behaving more like a digitalworker. And that's where the differencebetween generative AI and agentic AIbegins. Understanding this shift isimportant because it could shape thefuture of technology, jobs, andproductivity. So, in this video, we'llbreak down what generative AI is, whatagentic AI means, the key differencesbetween them, and why many expertsbelieve agentic AI is the next majorevolution of artificial intelligence.So, let's start with the AI most peoplealready know, generative AI. GenerativeAI is a type of artificial intelligencethat can create new content based onpatterns it has learned from massivedata sets. Instead of just analyzinginformation, it can actually generatenew outputs. These outputs can includetext, images, code, audio, and video.Some of the most popular generative AItools today include ChatGPT, Midjourney,and DALL-E. These tools can produceincredibly massive results in seconds.For example, you can ask AI These toolscan produce incredibly impressiveresults in seconds. For example, you canask AI to write an email, generatemarketing content, create an image,summarize a document, or even explain acomplex topic. And almost instantly, theAI produces an answer. But here is oneimportant limitation, generative AI isprompt-based. It only works when youtell it what to do. It doesn't takeinitiative, it doesn't plan task, and itdoesn't execute actions in the realworld. It simply generates an output inresponse to a prompt. And that's wherethe next evolution of AI begins.If generative AI is about creatingcontent, the next generation of AI isabout something much bigger. It's aboutgetting work done. And this brings us toagentic AI. Agentic AI refers to AIsystems that can act as autonomousagents. Instead of simply responding toprompts, these systems can set goals,break complex task into smaller steps,make decisions, use external tools, andexecute actions. In simple terms,generative AI answers questions, agenticAI solves problems.Let's look at a simple example. Imagineyou tell an AI plan a three-day trip toJapan under $1,500.A generative AI system might respondwith suggestions like places to visit,travel tips, hotel recommendations. Butan agentic AI system would go muchfurther. It could search for flights,compare hotel prices, build a completeitinerary, book reservations, and sendyou the final travel plan.All automatically. Instead of justsuggesting ideas, the AI actuallycompletes the task. That's the keydifference. So, what really separatesgenerative AI from agentic AI? Let'sbreak it down in simple terms. First,interaction. Generative AI isprompt-based. You ask a question and theAI gives a response. Agentic AI isgoal-based. You give it an objective andthe system figures out how to achieveit.Second, autonomy. Generative AI has lowautonomy. It depends completely on ouruser instructions. Agentic AI has higherautonomy. It can plan multiple steps andtake actions independently. Numberthree, execution. Generative AI mainlyproduces content. Agentic AI performsactions and executes workflows. Becauseof this, many experts believe agentic AIrepresents the next major phase ofartificial intelligence. Now, let's seewhy agentic AI is a big deal. The reasonagentic AI is getting so much attentionis because it moves AI from being just atool. It becomes a digital worker.Instead of helping with one small taskat a time, AI could start managingentire workflows. For example, inmarketing, an AI agent research a topic,write a blog post, generate images,schedule social media post, and analyzeengagement data. All automatically. Insoftware development, an AI agent couldanalyze product requirements, generatecode, test the code, detect bugs, anddeploy updates. This level of automationcould dramatically increase productivityacross industry. So, are we alreadyseeing agentic AI? The idea of agenticAI isn't just theoretical. We're alreadystarting to see early experiments.Projects like AutoGPT, BabyAGI areexamples of systems designed to operatewith minimal human input. These toolsuse powerful language models like thosebehind ChatGPT, but add extra layers toallow them to plan tasks, prioritizeactions, and execute workflows. Rightnow, these systems are stillexperimental, but they give us a glimpseof where AI development is heading. So,what is the future of AI? The future ofartificial intelligence will likelycombine both approaches. Generative AIwill continue to power creativity,content generation, explanation, andideas, while agentic AI will powerautomation, task execution, workflowmanagement. In other words, generativeAI creates the ideas, and agentic AIcarries them out.So, the difference between these twotypes of AI can be summarized simplyinto generative AI creates content, andagentic AI takes action. And as AIcontinues to evolve, we may soon seesystems that don't just answerquestions, but actually work alongsidehumans to complete complex tasks.Understanding this shift is importantbecause it could shape the nextgeneration of AI tools, businesses, andcareers.
